### 104. breathe <a id="104-breathe"></a>

**Etymology:** From Middle English *breathen*, from Old English *bræž* "breath, odor." The verb form developed from the noun, replacing earlier *ežian*. Related to *breath* and *brood*.

**词源：** 源自中古英语 *breathen*，来自古英语 *bræž*（呼吸、气味）。动词形式由名词发展而来，取代了更早的 *ežian*。与 *breath* 和 *brood* 同源。

**Definitions:**

- EN: To take air into and expel it from the lungs; to be alive
- CN: 呼吸；活着

**Part of Speech:** vi./vt.

**Usage Notes:**

- Can be intransitive (*breathe deeply*) or transitive (*breathe fresh air*) — 可不及物或及物
- Often used metaphorically (*breathe life into a project*) — 常用于比喻义
- Not to be confused with the noun *breath* — 注意与名词 breath 区分

**Common Collocations/Phrases:**

**General Collocations (English):**

- breathe deeply
- breathe heavily
- breathe in
- breathe out
- breathe air
- breathe oxygen
- breathe fire
- breathe a sigh of relief
- breathe life into
- breathe new life
- breathe freely
- breathe easily
- breathe through the nose
- barely breathe
- hard to breathe

- 吸入蒸汽以疏通鼻腔。

### 105. breed <a id="105-breed"></a>

**Etymology:** From Old English *brēdan* "to give birth, nourish, keep warm," from Proto-Germanic *brō*dijaną, from PIE *bhre- "to burn, heat." The sense of "produce offspring" developed from the warmth needed for hatching eggs.

**词源：** 源自古英语 *brēdan*（生育、滋养、保温），来自原始日耳曼语 *brō*dijaną，源自印欧语系 *bhre-（燃烧、加热）。“繁衍后代”之意源于孵化所需的热量。

**Definitions:**

- EN: To produce offspring; to raise animals; to cause or develop
- CN: 繁殖；饲养；孕育；导致

**Part of Speech:** vt./vi.

**Usage Notes:**

- Used for animals and plants, rarely for humans (humans *give birth* or *have children*) — 用于动植物，很少用于人类
- Metaphorically used for causing or developing conditions (*breed contempt*) — 比喻义表示导致或培养某种状况

**Common Collocations/Phrases:**

**General Collocations (English):**

- breed animals
- breed dogs
- breed horses
- breed cattle
- breed plants
- breed resistance
- breed contempt
- breed success
- breed violence
- breed disease
- breed bacteria
- breed suspicion
- selectively breed
- pure breed
- cross breed

<details>
<summary><b>General Collocations (中文)</b></summary>

- 饲养动物
- 养狗
- 养马
- 养牛
- 培育植物
- 培养抗性
- 招致轻蔑
- 孕育成功
- 滋生暴力
- 滋生疾病
- 培养细菌
- 招致怀疑
- 选择性培育
- 纯种
- 杂交
</details>

**Preposition Patterns (English):**

- breed + in [environment]
- breed + for [trait/purpose]

<details>
<summary><b>Preposition Patterns (中文)</b></summary>

- 在……环境中繁殖/滋生
- 为……特性/目的而培育
</details>

<summary>📖 EN Example Sentences</summary>

- 介词搭配: These horses were bred for speed and endurance.

### 106. brew <a id="106-brew"></a>

**Etymology:** From Old English *brēowan* "to brew," from Proto-Germanic *breuwaną, from PIE root *bhreu- "to boil, bubble." Related to *broth* and *bread*. Originally referred specifically to beer-making.

**词源：** 源自古英语 *brēowan*（酿造），来自原始日耳曼语 *breuwaną，源自印欧语系词根 *bhreu-（煮沸、冒泡）。与 *broth* 和 *bread* 同源。最初特指酿造啤酒。

**Definitions:**

- EN: To make beer or coffee by steeping, boiling, and fermenting; to develop or begin
- CN: 酿造；冲泡；酝酿

**Part of Speech:** vt./vi.

**Usage Notes:**

- Used for beer, coffee, and tea — 用于啤酒、咖啡和茶
- Metaphorically means something is developing (*trouble is brewing*) — 比喻义表示某事正在酝酿
- Can be used transitively (*brew beer*) or intransitively (*the storm is brewing*) — 既可及物也可不及物

**Common Collocations/Phrases:**

**General Collocations (English):**

- brew beer
- brew coffee
- brew tea
- brew a pot
- home brew
- brew a storm
- brew trouble
- brew a plan
- brew a plot
- brew a scheme
- let something brew
- freshly brewed
- brew house
- brew master

</details>

**Preposition Patterns (English):**

- brew + for [someone]
- brew + from [ingredient]

<summary><b>Preposition Patterns (中文)</b></summary>

- 为某人冲泡/酿造
- 用……原料酿造
</details>

### 107. bribe <a id="107-bribe"></a>

**Etymology:** From Old French *briber* "to beg, to ask for alms," of uncertain origin. The meaning shifted from "begging" to "giving money corruptly" in the 16th century.

**词源：** 源自古法语 *briber*（乞讨、求施舍），起源不确定。16世纪词义从“乞讨”转变为“行贿”。

**Definitions:**

- EN: To dishonestly give money or gifts to someone to influence their actions
- CN: 贿赂；向……行贿

**Part of Speech:** vt./vi.

**Usage Notes:**

- Strongly negative legal and ethical connotations — 具有强烈的负面法律和道德含义
- Can be used transitively (*bribe a judge*) or intransitively (*refuse to bribe*) — 可及物或不及物
- The noun form is *bribe* (the offering) or *bribery* (the practice) — 名词形式为 bribe（贿赂物）或 bribery（贿赂行为）

**Common Collocations/Phrases:**

**General Collocations (English):**

- bribe a judge
- bribe an official
- bribe a witness
- bribe a politician
- bribe a guard
- bribe one's way
- bribe voters
- accept a bribe
- offer a bribe
- take a bribe
- pay a bribe
- demand a bribe
- anti-bribery
- bribery scandal
- bribery charges

<summary><b>General Collocations (中文)</b></summary>

- 贿赂指控
</details>

**Preposition Patterns (English):**

- bribe + into [doing something]
- bribe + with [something]

<details>
<summary><b>Preposition Patterns (中文)</b></summary>

- 贿赂某人做某事
- 用……贿赂
</details>

<details open>
<summary>📖 EN Example Sentences</summary>

- 考研: The company tried to bribe the inspector into overlooking the safety violations. (考研英语 2014)

- Common: He bribed his way into the exclusive club.
- 介词搭配: The contractor bribed the building inspector into approving unsafe plans.
- 介词搭配: They bribed the security guard with a bottle of expensive whiskey.
</details>

### 108. bridge <a id="108-bridge"></a>

**Etymology:** From Old English *brycg* "bridge," from Proto-Germanic *brugjō*. The verb form emerged metaphorically, meaning "to build a bridge between" separate things or to connect gaps.

**词源：** 源自古英语 *brycg*（桥），来自原始日耳曼语 *brugjō*。动词形式以比喻义出现，意为“在……之间架桥”或弥合差距。

**Definitions:**

- EN: To reduce or eliminate the gap between two things; to connect
- CN: 弥合；连接；跨越

**Part of Speech:** vt.

**Usage Notes:**

- Almost always used metaphorically (gaps, differences, cultures) — 几乎总用于比喻义（差距、分歧、文化）
- *Bridge the gap* is the most common collocation — *bridge the gap* 是最常见的搭配

**Common Collocations/Phrases:**

**General Collocations (English):**

- bridge the gap
- bridge the divide
- bridge differences
- bridge cultures
- bridge worlds
- bridge the distance
- bridge the chasm
- bridge the generation gap
- bridge the digital divide
- bridge communication
- bridge understanding
- bridge the gulf
- bridge the separation

<summary><b>General Collocations (中文)</b></summary>

- 弥合分歧
- 消除分歧

- 跨越鸿沟
- 弥合代沟
- 弥合数字鸿沟
- 沟通
- 增进理解
- 跨越隔阂
- 消除分离
</details>

**Preposition Patterns (English):**

- bridge + between [two things]
- bridge + with [something]

<details>
<summary><b>Preposition Patterns (中文)</b></summary>

- 在……之间架起桥梁
- 用……弥合/连接
</details>

- TOEFL/IELTS: The organization works to bridge cultures through international exchange programs. (TOEFL TPO 41)

- 介词搭配: The project tries to bridge the gap between rural and urban education.

</details>

### 109. brief <a id="109-brief"></a>

**Etymology:** From Latin *brevis* "short, brief." The verb form developed from the noun *brief* (a concise statement), which came from legal Latin *brevis* "short document." The military meaning "to give instructions" emerged in the 20th century.

**词源：** 源自拉丁语 *brevis*（短的、简洁的）。动词形式由名词 *brief*（简要陈述）发展而来，该名词来自法律拉丁语 *brevis*（简短文件）。“下达指示”的军事含义出现于20世纪。

**Definitions:**

- EN: To give someone information or instructions about something before it happens
- CN: 向……介绍情况；给……下达指示

**Part of Speech:** vt.

**Usage Notes:**

- Common in legal, military, and business contexts — 常用于法律、军事和商业语境
- Often used in passive: *be briefed on something* — 常用被动形式：*be briefed on something*
- The opposite is *debrief* — 反义词为 *debrief*

**Common Collocations/Phrases:**

**General Collocations (English):**

- brief a team
- brief a client
- brief a lawyer
- brief the president
- brief the media
- brief soldiers
- brief staff
- brief on the situation
- brief about changes
- be fully briefed
- keep someone briefed
- legal brief
- briefing session
- briefing document
- pre-flight briefing

</details>

**Preposition Patterns (English):**

- brief + on [something]
- brief + about [something]

<details>
<summary><b>Preposition Patterns (中文)</b></summary>

- 就……向某人介绍情况
- 简要介绍……

### 110. brighten <a id="110-brighten"></a>

**Etymology:** From Middle English *brightenen*, from Old English *beorht* "bright" + the verb suffix *-en*. A native English formation meaning "to make or become bright."

**词源：** 源自中古英语 *brightenen*，来自古英语 *beorht*（明亮的）加动词后缀 *-en*。英语本土形成的词，意为“使变亮”或“变亮”。

**Definitions:**

- EN: To make or become brighter; to make more cheerful
- CN: 使变亮；变亮；使更快乐

**Part of Speech:** vt./vi.

**Usage Notes:**

- Can be both transitive (*brighten a room*) and intransitive (*the sky brightened*) — 既可及物也可不及物
- Commonly used emotionally (*brighten one's mood*) — 常用于情感/情绪方面

**Common Collocations/Phrases:**

- brighten a room
- brighten the sky

- brighten one's eyes

- brighten one's spirits
- brighten the outlook
- brighten the corner
- brighten with joy
- brighten with color
- brighten naturally

### 111. bring <a id="111-bring"></a>

**Etymology:** From Old English *bringan* "to bring, carry, convey," from Proto-Germanic *bringaną, from PIE *bʰrenk- "to bring." One of the core Germanic verbs with extensive phrasal verb usage.

**词源：** 源自古英语 *bringan*（带来、携带、运送），来自原始日耳曼语 *bringaną，源自印欧语系 *bʰrenk-（带来）。是日耳曼语系核心动词之一，有大量短语动词用法。

**Definitions:**

- EN: To carry or convey something to a place; to cause something to happen
- CN: 带来；携带；导致

**Part of Speech:** vt.

**Usage Notes:**

- The past tense and past participle are irregular: *brought* — 过去式和过去分词不规则：*brought*
- Extremely common in phrasal verbs (*bring up, bring about, bring in, bring out*, etc.) — 大量用于短语动词
- Unlike *take* (movement away), *bring* implies movement toward the speaker — 与 *take*（带离）不同，*bring* 暗示朝向说话者的移动

**Common Collocations/Phrases:**

**General Collocations (English):**

- bring someone
- bring something
- bring good news
- bring bad news
- bring joy
- bring happiness
- bring trouble
- bring change
- bring results
- bring success
- bring peace
- bring war
- bring an end
- bring to mind
- bring to light

<details>
<summary><b>General Collocations (中文)</b></summary>

- 带某人来
- 带来某物
- 带来好消息
- 带来坏消息

- 带来结果
- 带来成功
- 带来和平
- 带来战争
- 结束
- 使想起
- 揭露
</details>

**Preposition Patterns (English):**

- bring + to [someone/somewhere]
- bring + from [somewhere]
- bring + with [someone]
- bring + up [a topic]
- bring + about [something]
- bring + out [something]
- bring + in [something]
- bring + down [something/someone]

<details>

- 降低；击倒
</details>

<details open>
<summary>📖 EN Example Sentences</summary>

- 介词搭配: The medication brought down his fever quickly.
- 介词搭配: The company brought out a new version of the software.
</details>

### 112. broadcast <a id="112-broadcast"></a>

**Etymology:** From *broad* + *cast*, formed in English in the 18th century meaning "to scatter widely." First used in agriculture for sowing seeds, later adopted by radio in the 1920s.

**词源：** 由 *broad*（广泛的）和 *cast*（投掷）组合而成，18世纪在英语中形成，意为“广泛撒播”。最初用于农业播种，20世纪20年代被无线电领域采用。

**Definitions:**

- EN: To transmit a program or information via radio, television, or the internet
- CN: 广播；播送；传播

**Part of Speech:** vt./vi.

**Usage Notes:**

- The past tense and past participle are *broadcast* (also *broadcasted* is accepted but less common) — 过去式和过去分词为 *broadcast*（*broadcasted* 也可接受但较少用）
- Now also used for live-streaming and digital content — 现已用于直播和数字内容传播
- Can be used transitively (*broadcast a show*) or intransitively (*the station broadcasts 24/7*) — 可及物或不及物

**Common Collocations/Phrases:**

**General Collocations (English):**

- broadcast a program
- broadcast a show
- broadcast news
- broadcast live
- broadcast a game
- broadcast a speech
- broadcast a message
- broadcast an announcement
- broadcast on TV
- broadcast on radio
- broadcast online
- broadcast worldwide
- satellite broadcast
- live broadcast
- broadcast station

<details>
<summary><b>General Collocations (中文)</b></summary>

- 播送表演
- 播报新闻
- 现场直播
- 转播比赛
- 播送演讲
- 传播信息
- 发布公告
- 在电视上播出
- 在广播中播出
- 在线播出
- 全球播出
- 卫星转播
- 现场直播
- 广播电台
</details>

**Preposition Patterns (English):**

- broadcast + to [audience/place]
- broadcast + on [medium/channel]

<details>
<summary><b>Preposition Patterns (中文)</b></summary>

- 向……播出
- 通过……（频道/媒体）播出
</details>

<details open>
<summary>📖 EN Example Sentences</summary>

- CET-4/6: The concert was broadcast live to audiences around the world. (CET-4 2018-06)
- 考研: The president's address was broadcast on all major television networks. (考研英语 2014)
- TOEFL/IELTS: Many universities now broadcast their lectures online for remote students. (TOEFL TPO 44)
- Academic: The experiment's results were broadcast to research institutions globally. (Nature/2021)
- Common: The radio station broadcasts news every hour.
- 介词搭配: The event was broadcast to millions of viewers across the country.
- 介词搭配: The game will be broadcast on both television and the internet.
</details>

- 该活动向全国数百万观众播出。

### 113. broaden <a id="113-broaden"></a>

**Etymology:** From *broad* + *-en*, formed in English in the 18th century. A relatively recent formation compared to many other native English verbs.

**词源：** 由 *broad*（广阔的）加动词后缀 *-en* 构成，形成于18世纪。与许多其他本土英语动词相比，相对较新。

**Definitions:**

- EN: To become or make something wider or more extensive
- CN: 使变宽；拓宽；扩大

**Part of Speech:** vt./vi.

**Usage Notes:**

- Both transitive (*broaden one's horizons*) and intransitive (*the river broadens*) — 既可及物也可不及物
- Very commonly used with abstract nouns (*horizons, knowledge, experience, appeal*) — 常与抽象名词连用
- *Broaden one's horizons* is an extremely common idiom — *broaden one's horizons* 是极常见的习语

**Common Collocations/Phrases:**

**General Collocations (English):**

- broaden one's horizons
- broaden one's mind
- broaden one's perspective
- broaden one's experience
- broaden one's knowledge
- broaden one's outlook
- broaden one's understanding
- broaden one's view
- broaden the scope
- broaden the range
- broaden the appeal
- broaden the base
- broaden participation
- broaden access
- broaden a road

- broaden + into [something]
- broaden + from [something] to [something]

<summary><b>Preposition Patterns (中文)</b></summary>

- 扩展到……
- 从……扩展到……
</details>

- 介词搭配: The narrow path broadened into a wide road near the village.

### 114. broker <a id="114-broker"></a>

**Etymology:** From Anglo-Norman *brocour* "small trader," from Old French *brocheor* "wine retailer," from *broche* "spigot." The verb form developed from the noun, meaning "to act as a broker or intermediary."

**词源：** 源自盎格鲁-诺曼语 *brocour*（小商人），来自古法语 *brocheor*（葡萄酒零售商），源自 *broche*（塞子）。动词形式由名词发展而来，意为“充当中介或经纪人”。

**Definitions:**

- EN: To arrange or negotiate (an agreement, deal, or settlement) as an intermediary
- CN: 斡旋；协商；作为中间人安排

**Part of Speech:** vt.

**Usage Notes:**

- Formal usage, typically in politics, business, and diplomacy — 正式用语，常用于政治、商业和外交
- Strongly implies mediation between conflicting parties — 强烈暗示在冲突方之间进行调解
- Not to be confused with the noun *broker* — 注意与名词 broker 区分

**Common Collocations/Phrases:**

**General Collocations (English):**

- broker a deal
- broker an agreement
- broker a peace
- broker a ceasefire
- broker a settlement
- broker a truce
- broker a compromise
- broker a contract
- broker a merger
- broker a treaty
- broker a transaction
- broker a solution
- broker talks
- broker negotiations
- broker a meeting

- 安排会议
</details>

**Preposition Patterns (English):**

- broker + between [parties]
- broker + with [someone]

<details>
<summary><b>Preposition Patterns (中文)</b></summary>

- 在……之间斡旋
- 与……协商
</details>

<summary>📖 EN Example Sentences</summary>

- 介词搭配: The ambassador brokered a compromise between the two political parties.

</details>

### 115. brood <a id="115-brood"></a>

**Etymology:** From Old English *brō*d* "brood, offspring," from Proto-Germanic *brō*dą, from PIE *bhre- "to burn, heat." The verb sense of "to sit on eggs" came first, then extended to "to think deeply and moodily."

**词源：** 源自古英语 *brō*d*（一窝幼崽），来自原始日耳曼语 *brō*dą，源自印欧语系 *bhre-（燃烧、加热）。动词的“孵蛋”含义先出现，后扩展为“沉思、忧思”。

**Definitions:**

- EN: To think deeply about something that makes one unhappy; to sit on eggs to hatch them
- CN: 沉思；忧思；孵蛋

**Part of Speech:** vi./vt.

**Usage Notes:**

- Intransitive when meaning "to worry or think deeply" (*brood over/about*) — 表示“担忧或沉思”时为不及物动词
- Transitive when meaning "to sit on eggs" (*brood eggs*) — 表示“孵蛋”时为及物动词
- Negative connotation when used for thinking (suggests dwelling on problems) — 用于“思考”时带有负面含义（暗示纠结于问题）

**Common Collocations/Phrases:**

**General Collocations (English):**

- brood over
- brood about
- brood on
- brood silently
- brood alone
- brood constantly
- brood endlessly
- brood in silence
- brood in one's room
- brood over a problem
- brood over a loss
- brood over a mistake
- brood over the past
- brood hen
- brood eggs

<details>
<summary><b>General Collocations (中文)</b></summary>

- 担忧
- 沉思
- 默默沉思
- 独自忧思

- 沉默地忧思

- 为过错而懊悔
- 为过去而伤感
- 孵蛋的母鸡
- 孵蛋
</details>

**Preposition Patterns (English):**

- brood over [something]
- brood about [something]
- brood on [something]

<details>
<summary><b>Preposition Patterns (中文)</b></summary>

- 为……忧思/耿耿于怀
- 担心……
- 沉思……
</details>

<summary>📖 EN Example Sentences</summary>

- TOEFL/IELTS: Some bird species brood their eggs for several weeks before hatching. (TOEFL TPO 36)
- Academic: The chimp sat apart from the group, brooding on a separate branch. (Nature/2020)
- Common: Stop brooding over things you cannot change.
- 介词搭配: He brooded over the team's loss for days.
- 介词搭配: She brooded about the unfair criticism she received.
- 介词搭配: The old man brooded on the mistakes of his youth.
</details>

<details style="font-size: 13px !important">

### 116. browse <a id="116-browse"></a>

**Etymology:** From French *brouter* "to browse, to eat shoots," from Old French *brost* "bud, shoot." The sense of "casually looking through" (books, goods) emerged in the 19th century, extended digitally in the late 20th.

**词源：** 源自法语 *brouter*（吃嫩芽），来自古法语 *brost*（芽、嫩枝）。“随意翻阅”之意（书籍、商品）出现于19世纪，后扩展至数字领域。

**Definitions:**

- EN: To look at or read in a casual or leisurely way; to search for information on a computer
- CN: 浏览；翻阅；随意观看

**Part of Speech:** vi./vt.

**Usage Notes:**

- Very common in computing (*browse the internet, browse a website*) — 在计算机领域非常常见
- Can be transitive (*browse a store*) or intransitive (*browse through a magazine*) — 可及物或不及物
- Also used literally for animals eating leaves (*deer browsing*) — 也用于动物吃树叶（如鹿吃嫩叶）

**Common Collocations/Phrases:**

**General Collocations (English):**

- browse the internet
- browse a website
- browse online
- browse the web
- browse a store
- browse a catalog
- browse a magazine
- browse a book
- browse through
- browse around
- browse casually
- browse aimlessly
- browse for information
- browse for gifts
- browse securely

<details>

- 安全浏览
</details>

**Preposition Patterns (English):**

- browse through [something]
- browse for [something]

<details>
<summary><b>Preposition Patterns (中文)</b></summary>

- 翻阅……
- 寻找……而浏览
</details>

### 117. bruise <a id="117-bruise"></a>

**Etymology:** From Old English *brysan* "to crush, bruise," from Proto-Germanic *brusijaną. Influenced by Old French *bruiser* "to break." The word has been in English since before the Norman Conquest.

**词源：** 源自古英语 *brysan*（压碎、挫伤），来自原始日耳曼语 *brusijaną。受古法语 *bruiser*（打破）影响。该词在诺曼征服前就已存在于英语中。

**Definitions:**

- EN: To cause a bruise on skin or fruit; to become bruised
- CN: 使瘀青；碰伤；挫伤

**Part of Speech:** vt./vi.

**Usage Notes:**

- Can be transitive (*bruise one's arm*) or intransitive (*her skin bruises easily*) — 可及物或不及物
- Used for both living tissue and fruit/vegetables — 既用于活体组织也用于水果蔬菜
- Metaphorically means to hurt someone's feelings (*bruised ego*) — 比喻义表示伤害某人感情

**Common Collocations/Phrases:**

**General Collocations (English):**

- bruise easily
- bruise one's arm
- bruise one's leg
- bruise one's face
- bruise one's ego
- bruise one's pride
- bruise feelings
- bruised fruit
- bruised skin
- bruised tissue
- badly bruised
- deeply bruised
- emotionally bruised
- fresh bruise
- dark bruise

### 118. brush <a id="118-brush"></a>

**Etymology:** From Old French *broisse* "brush," from Medieval Latin *bruscia* "brushwood." The verb form emerged from the noun, with meanings extending from "to clean with a brush" to "to touch lightly in passing."

**词源：** 源自古法语 *broisse*（刷子），来自中世纪拉丁语 *bruscia*（灌木丛）。动词形式由名词发展而来，含义从“用刷子清洁”扩展到“轻轻擦过”。

**Definitions:**

- EN: To clean or smooth with a brush; to touch lightly in passing
- CN: 刷；擦；拂过；轻触

**Part of Speech:** vt./vi.

**Usage Notes:**

- Many phrasal verb meanings (*brush off, brush up on, brush aside*) — 有多种短语动词含义
- Can be literal (brushing teeth/hair) or metaphorical (brushing aside criticism) — 可指字面义或比喻义
- *Brush up on* means to review or improve skills — *brush up on* 指复习或提高技能

**Common Collocations/Phrases:**

**General Collocations (English):**

- brush one's teeth
- brush one's hair
- brush one's coat
- brush the floor
- brush away
- brush off
- brush aside
- brush past
- brush against
- brush up on
- brush with death
- brush stroke
- brush fire
- brush away tears
- brush off criticism

### 119. bubble <a id="119-bubble"></a>

**Etymology:** From Middle English *bobel* "a bubble," of imitative origin, mimicking the sound of bubbling water. The verb form developed in the 14th century.

**词源：** 源自中古英语 *bobel*（气泡），拟声起源，模仿水冒泡的声音。动词形式形成于14世纪。

**Definitions:**

- EN: To form or produce bubbles; to move with a bubbling sound
- CN: 起泡；冒泡；沸腾

**Part of Speech:** vi.

**Usage Notes:**

- Primarily intransitive — 主要为不及物动词
- Often used with *up* or *over* (*bubble up, bubble over*) — 常与 *up* 或 *over* 连用
- Metaphorically means to be full of emotion (*bubbling with excitement*) — 比喻义表示充满某种情绪

**General Collocations (English):**

### 120. buck <a id="120-buck"></a>

**Etymology:** From Old English *buc* "male deer," from Proto-Germanic *bukkaz. The verb sense "to jump up" derives from a male deer's leaping motion. The slang meaning "to resist" emerged in the 19th century.

**词源：** 源自古英语 *buc*（雄鹿），来自原始日耳曼语 *bukkaz。动词“跳起”之意源于雄鹿跳跃的动作。俚语中“抵制”的含义出现于19世纪。

**Definitions:**

- EN: To jump up with a back arched; to resist or oppose something
- CN: 弓背跳跃；抵制；反对

**Part of Speech:** vi./vt.

**Usage Notes:**

- Originally about horses trying to throw riders — 最初指马弓背跳跃试图甩下骑手
- *Buck the trend* is a common business idiom meaning to go against the general direction — *buck the trend* 是常见商业习语，意为逆势而行
- Also slang for dollar (*big bucks*) — 俚语中也指美元

**Common Collocations/Phrases:**

**General Collocations (English):**

- buck the trend
- buck the system
- buck authority
- buck convention
- buck tradition
- buck the odds
- buck expectations
- buck the market
- buck up
- buck off
- buck wildly
- buck hard
- try to buck
- buck and kick
- buck against

- 反抗
</details>

- buck + against [something]
- buck + up [someone]

- 使……振作起来
</details>

### 121. buckle <a id="121-buckle"></a>

**Etymology:** From Old French *bocle* "boss of a shield," later "metal ring for fastening," from Latin *buccula* "cheek strap of a helmet." The verb meaning "to fasten with a buckle" and "to bend under pressure" developed separately.

**词源：** 源自古法语 *bocle*（盾牌中心的凸起），后指“金属扣环”，来自拉丁语 *buccula*（头盔的颊带）。动词“用扣环扣住”和“受压弯曲”的含义各自独立发展。

**Definitions:**

- EN: To fasten with a buckle; to bend or collapse under pressure
- CN: 扣住；扣紧；弯曲；坍塌

**Part of Speech:** vt./vi.

**Usage Notes:**

- Two opposite meanings: "to fasten" (transitive) and "to collapse/bend" (intransitive) — 两个相反的含义：“扣住”（及物）和“坍塌/弯曲”（不及物）
- *Buckle up* is the common phrase for fastening a seatbelt — *buckle up* 是系安全带的常用短语
- *Buckle under pressure* means to give in — *buckle under pressure* 指在压力下屈服

**Common Collocations/Phrases:**

**General Collocations (English):**

- buckle up
- buckle a belt
- buckle a strap
- buckle a shoe
- buckle one's seatbelt
- buckle under pressure
- buckle under stress
- buckle under the weight
- buckle under strain
- buckle under the load
- knees buckle
- legs buckle
- bridge buckle
- pipe buckle
- buckle down

### 122. bud <a id="122-bud"></a>

**Etymology:** From Middle English *budde* "bud," of uncertain origin. The verb "to bud" developed in the 15th century from the noun, meaning "to produce buds." Related to Dutch *bot* and German *Butte*.

**词源：** 源自中古英语 *budde*（芽），起源不确定。动词“发芽”在15世纪由名词发展而来。与荷兰语 *bot* 和德语 *Butte* 相关。

**Definitions:**

- EN: To produce buds; to begin to grow or develop
- CN: 发芽；萌芽；开始发展

**Part of Speech:** vi.

**Usage Notes:**

- Primarily used for plants in spring — 主要用于描述植物在春天的生长
- Can be used metaphorically for ideas, relationships, or talents — 可用于比喻义，表示想法、关系或才能的萌芽
- Often used in participle form: *budding* (developing) — 常用分词形式：*budding*（发展中的）

**Common Collocations/Phrases:**

**General Collocations (English):**

- bud in spring
- bud early
- bud late
- begin to bud
- start to bud
- trees budding
- flowers budding
- buds appear
- bud opening
- newly budded
- budding artist
- budding romance
- budding talent
- budding relationship
- budding entrepreneur

### 123. budget <a id="123-budget"></a>

**Etymology:** From Middle English *bougette* "leather bag," from French *bougette*, diminutive of *bouge* "bag." The meaning shifted from "bag" to "contents of a bag" to "financial plan" in the 18th century.

**词源：** 源自中古英语 *bougette*（皮袋），来自法语 *bougette*，*bouge*（袋子）的指小形式。含义从“袋子”演变为“袋中的东西”，再到18世纪的“财务计划”。

**Definitions:**

- EN: To plan how to spend money; to allocate financial resources
- CN: 制定预算；规划开支

**Part of Speech:** vt./vi.

**Usage Notes:**

- Commonly used in personal finance, business, and government contexts — 常用于个人理财、企业和政府语境
- Can be transitive (*budget money for something*) or intransitive (*we need to budget carefully*) — 可及物或不及物
- *Budget for* means to plan for expected costs — *budget for* 意为为预期成本做规划

**Common Collocations/Phrases:**

**General Collocations (English):**

- budget money
- budget time
- budget resources
- budget carefully
- budget wisely
- budget tightly
- budget for a trip
- budget for a project
- budget for emergencies
- budget one's income
- budget one's expenses
- annual budget
- monthly budget
- tight budget
- budget deficit

</details>

- budget for [something]
- budget + at [amount]

- 预算为……金额
</details>

- 介词搭配: The project was budgeted at approximately $2 million.

### 124. buffer <a id="124-buffer"></a>

**Etymology:** From *buff* (a soft leather) + *-er*, from Old French *buffe* "a blow." The meaning "something that cushions" (mechanical) dates from the 19th century; the computing sense dates from the 20th.

**词源：** 源自 *buff*（软皮）加 *-er*，来自古法语 *buffe*（一击）。“缓冲物”的机械含义始于19世纪；计算机领域的含义始于20世纪。

**Definitions:**

- EN: To protect or lessen the impact of something; to store data temporarily
- CN: 缓冲；减轻冲击；暂存数据

**Part of Speech:** vt.

**Usage Notes:**

- Used in physical, chemical, and computing contexts — 用于物理、化学和计算机领域
- *Buffer zone* is a common term — *buffer zone* 是常见术语
- In chemistry, a buffer solution resists pH change — 在化学中，缓冲液抵抗pH值变化

**Common Collocations/Phrases:**

**General Collocations (English):**

- buffer the impact
- buffer the blow
- buffer the effect
- buffer against loss
- buffer against shock
- buffer zone
- buffer solution
- buffer capacity
- buffer state
- buffer stock
- buffer data
- buffer memory
- buffer size
- provide a buffer
- act as a buffer

<summary><b>General Collocations (中文)</b></summary>

- 缓冲效果

</details>

- buffer + from [something]

- Academic: The protein solution buffers the cells against changes in pH. (Nature/2021)
- Common: The software buffers the video before playing it smoothly.
- 介词搭配: Savings can buffer you against unexpected job loss.
- 介词搭配: The insulation buffers the room from outside noise.
</details>

### 125. buffet <a id="125-buffet"></a>

**Etymology:** From Old French *buffeter* "to strike with the fist," from *buffe* "a blow." The noun meaning "sideboard" has a separate origin from French *bufet*. The verb retains the sense of "striking repeatedly."

**词源：** 源自古法语 *buffeter*（用拳头打），来自 *buffe*（一击）。名词“餐台”的来源不同。动词保留了“反复击打”的含义。

**Definitions:**

- EN: To strike repeatedly; to knock or push roughly
- CN: 反复打击；冲击；折磨

**Part of Speech:** vt.

**Usage Notes:**

- Almost always used in passive voice or with natural forces as the subject — 几乎总是用于被动语态或以自然力量为主语
- Common in weather descriptions (*buffeted by winds*) — 常见于天气描述（如被风吹打）
- Not to be confused with the noun *buffet* (自助餐) which is pronounced differently — 注意与名词 buffet（自助餐）区分

**Common Collocations/Phrases:**

**General Collocations (English):**

- buffeted by the wind
- buffeted by storms
- buffeted by waves
- buffeted by fate
- buffeted by criticism
- buffeted by misfortune
- buffeted by the economy
- buffeted by hardship
- buffeted about
- buffeted around
- wind-buffeted
- storm-buffeted
- wave-buffeted
- buffeted coast
- buffeted region

- 受风暴袭击的

- 受冲击的海岸
- 受冲击的地区
</details>

**Preposition Patterns (English):**

- buffet + by [something]
- buffet + against [something]

- 被……打击/冲击
- 撞击……

### 126. bug <a id="126-bug"></a>

**Etymology:** From Middle English *bugge* "a frightening thing, ghost," of uncertain origin. The modern meaning "insect" emerged in the 17th century. The verb senses—"to annoy" and "to eavesdrop with a device"—both developed in the 20th century.

**词源：** 源自中古英语 *bugge*（吓人的东西、鬼怪），起源不确定。现代“昆虫”之意出现于17世纪。动词“烦扰”和“用窃听器窃听”均发展于20世纪。

**Definitions:**

- EN: To annoy or irritate someone; to install a hidden listening device
- CN: 烦扰；使恼怒；装窃听器

**Part of Speech:** vt.

**Usage Notes:**

- Informal when meaning "to annoy" — 表示“烦扰”时为非正式用法
- Technical when meaning "to install a bug (listening device)" — 表示“安装窃听器”时为技术性用法

**Common Collocations/Phrases:**

**General Collocations (English):**

- bug me
- bug the room
- bug the phone
- bug the office
- bug the conversation
- stop bugging me
- really bug
- kind of bug
- bug detector
- bugging device
- plant a bug
- sweep for bugs
- spy bug
- audio bug

</details>

</details>

### 127. build <a id="127-build"></a>

**Etymology:** From Old English *byldan* "to build a house," from *bold* "house," from Proto-Germanic *buþ*lą. Related to *bower* and *booth*. The word has remained central to English construction vocabulary since Anglo-Saxon times.

**词源：** 源自古英语 *byldan*（建造房屋），来自 *bold*（房屋），源自原始日耳曼语 *buþ*lą。与 *bower* 和 *booth* 相关。自盎格鲁-撒克逊时代以来一直是英语建造词汇的核心。

**Definitions:**

- EN: To construct something by assembling parts; to develop or create
- CN: 建造；修建；建立；发展

**Part of Speech:** vt./vi.

**Usage Notes:**

- Can be used literally (build a house) or metaphorically (build a relationship) — 可字面义或比喻义
- The past tense and past participle are irregular: *built* — 过去式和过去分词不规则：*built*
- Extremely productive in phrasal verbs (*build up, build on, build in*) — 大量用于短语动词

**Common Collocations/Phrases:**

**General Collocations (English):**

- build a house
- build a bridge
- build a road
- build a wall
- build a career
- build a relationship
- build trust
- build confidence
- build a team
- build a business
- build an empire
- build on success
- build upon
- build up
- build from scratch

- 积累；增强
- 从零开始建立
</details>

- build on/upon [something]
- build + into [something]
- build + around [something]
- build + up [something]
- build + out of [material]

### 128. bully <a id="128-bully"></a>

**Etymology:** From Dutch *boel* "lover, brother," later used as a term of endearment. The meaning degraded from "fine fellow" to "swaggering coward" in the 17th century, then to "one who intimidates" in the 18th.

**词源：** 源自荷兰语 *boel*（情人、兄弟），后用作昵称。词义从“好伙计”恶化为17世纪的“虚张声势的懦夫”，18世纪进一步演变为“恃强凌弱者”。

**Definitions:**

- EN: To use strength or influence to harm or intimidate someone weaker
- CN: 欺凌；恐吓；欺负

**Part of Speech:** vt./vi.

**Usage Notes:**

- Strongly negative connotation, especially in school and workplace contexts — 强烈的负面含义，尤用于学校和工作场所
- Has become a major social issue (*anti-bullying campaigns*) — 已成为重要的社会议题
- Can be transitive (*bully someone*) or intransitive (*stop bullying*) — 可及物或不及物

**Common Collocations/Phrases:**

**General Collocations (English):**

- bully someone
- bully a classmate
- bully a coworker
- bully the weak
- bully into submission
- bullied at school
- bullied online
- cyber bully
- bully victim
- anti-bullying
- bullying behavior
- bullied mercilessly
- bully relentlessly
- physical bullying
- verbal bullying

<details>
<summary><b>General Collocations (中文)</b></summary>

- 在网上被欺凌
- 网络欺凌

- 被无情欺凌
- 无情地欺凌
- 身体欺凌
- 言语欺凌
</details>

**Preposition Patterns (English):**

- bully + into [doing something]

<details>
<summary><b>Preposition Patterns (中文)</b></summary>

- 强迫某人做某事
</details>

- 考研: The older students bullied the younger ones into handing over their lunch money. (考研英语 2014)
- TOEFL/IELTS: Cyber bullying has become a serious concern with the rise of social media. (TOEFL TPO 42)

</details>

### 129. bump <a id="129-bump"></a>

**Etymology:** From Middle English *bumpen* "to strike," of imitative origin, mimicking the sound of a collision. First recorded in the 16th century.

**词源：** 源自中古英语 *bumpen*（撞击），拟声起源，模仿碰撞的声音。最早记载于16世纪。

**Definitions:**

- EN: To hit or knock against something; to move with jolts
- CN: 碰撞；颠簸行进

**Part of Speech:** vt./vi.

**Usage Notes:**

- Can be physical (*bump one's head*) or metaphorical (*bump into an old friend*) — 可指物理碰撞或比喻义（偶遇）
- *Bump into* means to meet unexpectedly — *bump into* 意为意外遇见
- *Bump up* means to increase — *bump up* 意为增加

**Common Collocations/Phrases:**

**General Collocations (English):**

- bump one's elbow
- bump into someone
- bump into something

- bump along
- bump up
- bump off

- bump gently
- bump hard

</details>

### 130. bunch <a id="130-bunch"></a>

**Etymology:** From Middle English *bunche* "a bundle," of uncertain origin, possibly from Dutch *bond* "bundle." The verb "to gather into a bunch" developed from the noun in the 16th century.

**词源：** 源自中古英语 *bunche*（一捆），起源不确定，可能来自荷兰语 *bond*（捆）。动词“聚集成束”由名词在16世纪发展而来。

**Definitions:**

- EN: To gather or form into a cluster or group
- CN: 使成束；使成群；聚集

**Part of Speech:** vt./vi.

**Usage Notes:**

- Often used with *up* or *together* — 常与 *up* 或 *together* 连用
- Can refer to people gathering closely or objects being gathered — 可指人聚集或物被收拢
- The noun form is very common (*a bunch of flowers*) — 名词形式很常见

### 131. bundle <a id="131-bundle"></a>

**Etymology:** From Middle Dutch *bundel* "bundle," from *binde* "to bind." Related to *bind* and *band*. The verb form emerged in the 17th century.

**词源：** 源自中古荷兰语 *bundel*（捆），来自 *binde*（捆绑）。与 *bind* 和 *band* 相关。动词形式出现于17世纪。

**Definitions:**

- EN: To tie or wrap something into a bundle; to sell items together as a package
- CN: 捆扎；打包；捆绑销售

**Part of Speech:** vt./vi.

**Usage Notes:**

- Physical meaning: to tie things together — 物理含义：将物品捆在一起
- Commercial meaning: to sell products together at a discount — 商业含义：将产品捆绑销售
- *Bundle up* means to dress warmly or to wrap up — *bundle up* 意为穿暖和或包裹起来

**Common Collocations/Phrases:**

**General Collocations (English):**

- bundle up
- bundle together
- bundle off
- bundle out
- bundle into
- bundle of clothes
- bundle of sticks
- bundle of cash
- bundle of nerves
- software bundle
- product bundle
- bundle of joy
- bundle tightly

### 132. burden <a id="132-burden"></a>

**Etymology:** From Old English *byrthen* "a load, weight," from Proto-Germanic *burthinjo, from PIE *bher- "to carry." The verb form developed directly from the noun.

**词源：** 源自古英语 *byrthen*（负担、重量），来自原始日耳曼语 *burthinjo，源自印欧语系 *bher-（背负）。动词形式由名词直接发展而来。

**Definitions:**

- EN: To cause someone to carry a heavy load or worry
- CN: 使负担；使负重担

**Part of Speech:** vt.

**Usage Notes:**

- Can be physical (*burdened with heavy bags*) or emotional (*burdened with guilt*) — 可指物理负担或情感负担
- Often used in passive voice — 常用于被动语态
- More formal than *weigh down* — 比 *weigh down* 更正式

**Common Collocations/Phrases:**

**General Collocations (English):**

- burden someone
- burden with debt
- burden with responsibility
- burden with guilt
- burden with worry
- burden with taxes
- burden with work
- burden the economy
- burden the system
- burden the environment
- heavily burdened
- financially burdened
- emotionally burdened
- unfairly burdened
- burden of proof

- burden + with [something]
- burden + by [something]

### 133. burn <a id="133-burn"></a>

**Etymology:** From Old English *byrnan* (intransitive) and *bærnan* (transitive), from Proto-Germanic *brinnaną and *brannijaną. From PIE *gwher- "to burn." One of the most fundamental verbs in the Germanic languages.

**词源：** 源自古英语 *byrnan*（不及物）和 *bærnan*（及物），来自原始日耳曼语 *brinnaną 和 *brannijaną。源自印欧语系 *gwher-（燃烧）。是日耳曼语系中最基本的动词之一。

**Definitions:**

- EN: To be on fire; to damage or destroy with fire; to use energy
- CN: 燃烧；烧毁；消耗

**Part of Speech:** vt./vi.

**Usage Notes:**

- Both transitive (*burn paper*) and intransitive (*the wood burns*) — 既可及物也可不及物
- Many metaphorical uses (*burn with passion, burn calories, burn bridges*) — 大量比喻用法
- Past tense and past participle: *burned* (US) or *burnt* (UK) — 过去式和过去分词：burned（美式）或 burnt（英式）

**Common Collocations/Phrases:**

**General Collocations (English):**

- burn down
- burn up
- burn out
- burn off
- burn calories
- burn energy
- burn fuel
- burn fat
- burn bridges
- burn one's bridges
- burn the midnight oil
- burn with curiosity
- burn with anger
- burn with passion
- burn to the ground

### 134. burst <a id="134-burst"></a>

**Etymology:** From Old English *berstan* "to break suddenly," from Proto-Germanic *brestaną. Related to *break* and *breach*. The word has remained remarkably stable in form and meaning since Anglo-Saxon times.

**词源：** 源自古英语 *berstan*（突然破裂），来自原始日耳曼语 *brestaną。与 *break* 和 *breach* 相关。该词的形式和含义自盎格鲁-撒克逊时代以来一直非常稳定。

**Definitions:**

- EN: To break open or apart suddenly and violently
- CN: 爆裂；突发；充满

**Part of Speech:** vi./vt.

**Usage Notes:**

- Past tense and past participle: *burst* (unchanged) — 过去式和过去分词不变：仍为 *burst*
- Intransitive: *balloon bursts* — 不及物：气球爆裂
- Transitive: *burst the balloon* — 及物：弄破气球

**Common Collocations/Phrases:**

**General Collocations (English):**

- burst into laughter
- burst into flames

- burst out crying
- burst open
- burst through
- burst in

- burst with joy
- burst with excitement
- burst a balloon
- burst a pipe

- burst at the seams

### 135. bury <a id="135-bury"></a>

**Etymology:** From Old English *byrgan* "to bury, inter," from Proto-Germanic *burgijaną, from PIE *bhergh- "to protect, hide." Related to *burial* and *burrow*.

**词源：** 源自古英语 *byrgan*（埋葬），来自原始日耳曼语 *burgijaną，源自印欧语系 *bhergh-（保护、隐藏）。与 *burial* 和 *burrow* 相关。

**Definitions:**

- EN: To put a dead body in the ground; to hide or cover something
- CN: 埋葬；掩埋；隐藏

**Part of Speech:** vt.

**Usage Notes:**

- Past tense and past participle: *buried* — 过去式和过去分词：*buried*
- Can be literal (*bury a body*) or metaphorical (*bury the hatchet, bury one's face*) — 可字面义或比喻义
- *Bury the hatchet* means to make peace — *bury the hatchet* 意为和解

**Common Collocations/Phrases:**

**General Collocations (English):**

- bury a body
- bury the dead
- bury treasure
- bury bones

- bury one's head
- bury one's feelings

- buried deep

### 136. bust <a id="136-bust"></a>

**Etymology:** A colloquial variant of *burst* from the 18th century, later influenced by *bust* (a sculpture of the head and shoulders from French *buste*). The sense "to arrest" or "to raid" emerged in American English in the 20th century.

**词源：** 18世纪 *burst* 的口语变体，后受法语 *buste*（半身像）的影响。“逮捕”或“搜查”之意在20世纪美国英语中形成。

**Definitions:**

- EN: To break, smash, or burst; to arrest or raid
- CN: 打破；弄坏；逮捕；搜查

**Part of Speech:** vt./vi.

**Usage Notes:**

- Informal in most senses — 大多为非正式用法
- *Bust* (n.) also means a failure (*the project was a bust*) — 名词也指失败
- *Bust up* means to destroy or end a relationship — *bust up* 意为破坏或结束关系

**Common Collocations/Phrases:**

**General Collocations (English):**

- bust a lock
- bust a pipe
- bust a balloon
- bust open
- bust up
- bust out
- bust into
- drug bust
- police bust
- bust a criminal
- bust a gang
- bust a myth
- bust a move
- boom or bust

### 137. butcher <a id="137-butcher"></a>

**Etymology:** From Old French *bouchier* "one who slaughters goats," from *boc* "he-goat," from Latin *buccus* "buck." The verb "to slaughter" developed from the noun in the 16th century.

**词源：** 源自古法语 *bouchier*（宰山羊的人），来自 *boc*（公山羊），源自拉丁语 *buccus*（雄鹿）。动词“屠宰”由名词在16世纪发展而来。

**Definitions:**

- EN: To slaughter or cut up an animal for food; to ruin something through incompetence
- CN: 屠宰；残杀；搞砸

**Part of Speech:** vt.

**Usage Notes:**

- Literal meaning: to kill and prepare animals for meat — 字面义：宰杀并准备肉类
- Figurative meaning: to ruin something or do a very poor job — 比喻义：搞砸或做得极差
- Strongly negative in figurative use — 比喻用法中具有强烈负面含义

**Common Collocations/Phrases:**

**General Collocations (English):**

- butcher meat
- butcher a cow
- butcher a pig
- butcher a chicken
- butcher a speech
- butcher a song
- butcher a play
- butcher a language
- butcher the job
- butcher the performance
- butcher an innocent
- butcher shop
- butcher knife
- butcher block
- butcher paper

### 138. butt <a id="138-butt"></a>

**Etymology:** From Old French *buter* "to strike against, to push," from Frankish *bōtan* "to strike." Related to *butt* (a target) and *butt* (cigarette end). The verb has carried the core meaning of "striking with the head" since the Middle Ages.

**词源：** 源自古法语 *buter*（撞击、推），来自法兰克语 *bōtan*（击打）。与名词 *butt*（靶子）和 *butt*（烟蒂）相关。中世纪以来一直保留“用头撞击”的核心含义。

**Definitions:**

- EN: To strike or push with the head or horns
- CN: 用头撞；用角顶

**Part of Speech:** vt./vi.

**Usage Notes:**

- Primarily used for animals (goats, bulls) — 主要用于动物（山羊、公牛）
- *Butt in* means to interrupt rudely — *butt in* 意为粗鲁地打断
- *Butt heads* means to disagree or argue — *butt heads* 意为发生分歧或争吵

**Common Collocations/Phrases:**

**General Collocations (English):**

- butt heads
- butt in
- butt out
- butt against
- goat butts
- ram butts
- bull butts
- butt someone
- butt into conversation
- butt into someone's business
- stop butting in
- butt roughly
- butt playfully
- butt stubbornly
- head butt

- 固执地顶撞
- 用头撞
</details>

- butt + against [something]
- butt + in on [something]

- 打断/干涉……

- 介词搭配: He butted in on their private discussion.

### 139. butter <a id="139-butter"></a>

**Etymology:** From Old English *butere* "butter," from Latin *butyrum*, from Greek *boutyron* "cow cheese." The verb "to spread butter" developed from the noun. *Butter up* (to flatter) emerged in the 19th century.

**词源：** 源自古英语 *butere*（黄油），来自拉丁语 *butyrum*，源自希腊语 *boutyron*（牛油、奶酪）。动词“涂黄油”由名词发展而来。*butter up*（奉承）出现在19世纪。

**Definitions:**

- EN: To spread butter on something; to flatter someone excessively
- CN: 涂黄油；奉承；巴结

**Part of Speech:** vt.

**Usage Notes:**

- Literal meaning: to spread butter on bread, toast, etc. — 字面义：在面包、吐司等上涂黄油
- Figurative meaning: *butter up* means to flatter someone to gain favor — 比喻义：*butter up* 意为奉承以博取好感

**Common Collocations/Phrases:**

**General Collocations (English):**

- butter bread
- butter toast
- butter a pan
- butter a dish
- butter generously
- butter lightly
- butter both sides
- butter up
- butter someone up
- butter up the boss
- butter up the teacher
- melted butter
- browned butter
- butter knife
- butter dish

### 140. button <a id="140-button"></a>

**Etymology:** From Old French *boton* "a button," from *boter* "to push," from Frankish *bōtan* "to strike." The verb "to fasten with buttons" emerged in the 14th century. The computing sense "to press a button" developed in the 20th century.

**词源：** 源自古法语 *boton*（纽扣），来自 *boter*（推），源自法兰克语 *bōtan*（击打）。“用扣子扣住”的动词含义出现在14世纪。计算机领域的“按按钮”之意在20世纪形成。

**Definitions:**

- EN: To fasten with buttons; to push a button
- CN: 扣上纽扣；按键

**Part of Speech:** vt./vi.

**Usage Notes:**

- Opposite is *unbutton* — 反义词为 *unbutton*
- *Button up* means to fasten all buttons; also means to complete details — *button up* 意为扣好所有扣子；也意为完成细节

**Common Collocations/Phrases:**

**General Collocations (English):**

- button a coat

- button down

- button it
- button the collar
- button the cuffs
- button carefully
- button incorrectly
- button all the way
- button tightly
- button loosely
- button front
- button fly

### 141. buy <a id="141-buy"></a>

**Etymology:** From Old English *bycgan* "to buy, pay for," from Proto-Germanic *bugjaną. Related to Old Norse *byggja* "to lend." The word has been a core commercial verb since Anglo-Saxon times with an irregular past tense (*bought*).

**词源：** 源自古英语 *bycgan*（购买、支付），来自原始日耳曼语 *bugjaną。与古诺尔斯语 *byggja*（借出）相关。自盎格鲁-撒克逊时代以来一直是商业核心动词，有不规则过去式 *bought*。

**Definitions:**

- EN: To obtain something in exchange for money; to believe or accept
- CN: 购买；相信；接受

**Part of Speech:** vt./vi.

**Usage Notes:**

- Past tense and past participle: *bought* — 过去式和过去分词：*bought*
- Idiomatic meaning: *I don't buy that* means "I don't believe that" — 俚语义：*I don't buy that* 意为“我不相信”
- Many phrasal verbs (*buy up, buy out, buy into, buy off*) — 大量短语动词
- Opposite: *sell* — 反义词：*sell*

**Common Collocations/Phrases:**

**General Collocations (English):**

- buy a house
- buy a car
- buy food
- buy clothes
- buy online
- buy in bulk
- buy on credit
- buy on impulse
- buy on sale
- buy time
- buy peace
- buy freedom
- buy off
- buy out
- buy into

### 142. buzz <a id="142-buzz"></a>

**Etymology:** From Middle English *buzzen*, of imitative origin, mimicking the sound of a bee or insect. The word has been used since the 14th century to describe buzzing sounds, and later extended to human activity and excitement.

**词源：** 源自中古英语 *buzzen*，拟声起源，模仿蜜蜂或昆虫的嗡嗡声。自14世纪以来用于描述嗡嗡声，后扩展至人类活动和兴奋状态。

**Definitions:**

- EN: To make a continuous low humming sound; to move quickly or excitedly
- CN: 嗡嗡叫；忙碌；充满兴奋

**Part of Speech:** vi./vt.

**Usage Notes:**

- Primarily intransitive for sounds (*bees buzz*) — 表示声音时主要为不及物
- Transitive in *buzz someone in* (to let someone enter via a buzzer) — *buzz someone in* 为及物用法
- *Buzz with* means to be full of activity or excitement — *buzz with* 意为充满活动或兴奋

**Common Collocations/Phrases:**

**General Collocations (English):**

- buzz around
- buzz with activity
- buzz with excitement
- buzz with rumors
- buzz with energy
- buzz with ideas
- buzz in
- buzz off
- buzz about
- bees buzz
- phone buzzes
- alarm buzzes
- buzzer buzzes
- buzz happily
- buzz loudly

<summary><b>General Collocations (中文)</b></summary>

- 大声嗡嗡
</details>

- buzz + around [place]
- buzz + about [something]
- buzz + in/into [place]

### 143. bypass <a id="143-bypass"></a>

**Etymology:** From *by* + *pass*, formed in English in the 19th century as a noun meaning "a secondary road." The verb "to go around" developed shortly after, and the medical sense (heart bypass) emerged in the 20th century.

**词源：** 由 *by*（经过）和 *pass*（通过）组合而成，19世纪在英语中形成，作为名词意为“旁路”。动词“绕行”之意稍后出现，医学上的“搭桥”之意在20世纪形成。

**Definitions:**

- EN: To go around or avoid an obstacle or procedure
- CN: 绕开；绕过；规避

**Part of Speech:** vt.

**Usage Notes:**

- Commonly used in traffic (*bypass a city*) — 常用于交通语境
- Medical: *bypass surgery* (heart bypass) — 医学：搭桥手术
- Technical/computing: *bypass security* — 技术/计算：绕过安全措施

**Common Collocations/Phrases:**

**General Collocations (English):**

- bypass a city
- bypass a town
- bypass traffic
- bypass an obstacle
- bypass a problem
- bypass security
- bypass a rule
- bypass a regulation
- bypass a procedure
- bypass a step
- bypass authority
- bypass the system
- bypass surgery
- heart bypass
- bypass road

- bypass + around [something]
